# ROC Curve Analysis for CIFAR-10 CNN Experiments

This notebook computes ROC (Receiver Operating Characteristic) curves and AUC (Area Under Curve) scores for CIFAR-10 CNN classification experiments. It compares model performance across different training configurations.

## Workflow

1. Load prediction probability files from DerivaML catalog as assets
2. Retrieve ground truth labels from the Image_Classification feature table
3. Compute per-class and micro/macro-averaged ROC curves
4. Generate confusion matrices
5. Save output assets (ROC curve JPEGs, confusion matrix JPEGs, metrics CSV)

## Output Assets

- `roc_curves_<experiment>_<asset_rid>.jpg` — Per-experiment ROC curve plots
- `confusion_matrix_<experiment>_<asset_rid>.jpg` — Per-experiment confusion matrices
- `roc_comparison_<analysis_execution_rid>.jpg` — Cross-experiment micro-AUC overlay (multi-experiment runs only)
- `roc_metrics.csv` — AUC scores, accuracy, and source `Asset_RID` for all experiments

The per-experiment files include the prediction CSV's asset RID so a hyperparameter sweep
(where every cell shares the same Hydra `model_config` name) doesn't silently overwrite
outputs.

## Requirements

- Prediction probability CSV files with columns: `Image_RID`, `Predicted_Class`, `prob_<classname>` for each class
- Ground truth labels stored in the Image_Classification feature (from a labeling execution with no confidence scores)


```python
import pandas as pd
import numpy as np
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
from sklearn.metrics import roc_curve, auc
from sklearn.preprocessing import label_binarize

from deriva_ml.execution import run_notebook
from deriva_ml.core.enums import MLAsset, ExecAssetType
```

## Initialize Notebook

Initialize the notebook with DerivaML execution context. This single call:
1. Loads all configuration modules
2. Resolves the hydra-zen configuration
3. Creates the DerivaML connection
4. Creates a workflow and execution context
5. Downloads any specified assets

Override configuration at runtime:
```python
ml, execution, config = run_notebook(
    "roc_analysis",
    overrides=["assets=roc_quick_probabilities"],  # Analyze single experiment
)
```

Available asset configurations (see `src/configs/assets.py`):
- `roc_quick_probabilities` - cifar10_quick experiment only
- `roc_extended_probabilities` - cifar10_extended experiment only  
- `roc_comparison_probabilities` - both experiments (default)


```python
# Initialize notebook - this single call handles all setup
ml, execution, config = run_notebook("roc_analysis", workflow_type="ROC Analysis Notebook")

from IPython.display import display, Markdown

display(Markdown(f"""**Connection:** `{ml.host_name}`, catalog `{ml.catalog_id}`

**Execution:** [{execution.execution_rid}]({ml.cite(execution.execution_rid)})

**Configuration:**
- Assets: `{config.assets}`
- Show per-class curves: `{config.show_per_class}`
- Confidence threshold: `{config.confidence_threshold}`

**Downloaded:** {list(execution.asset_paths.keys())}
"""))

# Display asset configuration description
if hasattr(config.assets, 'description') and config.assets.description:
    display(Markdown(f"**Asset Configuration Description:**\n\n{config.assets.description}"))
```

## Load Experiments from Assets

Load the prediction probability CSV files from downloaded assets and create `Experiment` objects to access configuration metadata.


```python
from pathlib import Path

from IPython.display import display, Markdown, HTML

# Build Experiment objects for each prediction asset.
#
# Each experiment is downloaded into its own per-RID subdirectory so
# multiple experiments can coexist on disk without their CSVs sharing a
# path. We then load each experiment's CSV from its own directory and
# verify (md5) that no two experiments produced byte-identical content,
# which would indicate something is collapsing distinct experiments
# into the same data.

per_rid_dir = Path(execution.working_dir) / 'per_asset_downloads'
per_rid_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

experiments = []
loaded_info = []

for asset_path in execution.asset_paths.get('Execution_Asset', []):
    if asset_path.file_name.name != 'prediction_probabilities.csv':
        continue

    asset = ml.lookup_asset(asset_path.asset_rid)
    asset_executions = asset.list_executions(asset_role='Output')
    if not asset_executions:
        continue

    exec_rid = asset_executions[0].execution_rid
    exp = ml.lookup_experiment(exec_rid)

    # Download into a per-RID subdirectory and read from there. Going
    # through execution.download_asset keeps us on the credential-aware
    # Hatrac path the rest of the execution uses.
    rid_dir = per_rid_dir / asset_path.asset_rid
    rid_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    fresh_path = execution.download_asset(
        asset_rid=asset_path.asset_rid,
        dest_dir=rid_dir,
        update_catalog=False,
    )
    df = pd.read_csv(fresh_path)

    experiments.append({
        'experiment': exp,
        'asset_rid': asset_path.asset_rid,
        'csv_path': fresh_path,
        'data': df,
        'name': exp.name,
        'config_choices': exp.config_choices,
        'model_config': exp.model_config,
    })
    loaded_info.append(
        f"- **{exp.name}**: {len(df)} predictions "
        f"(execution [{exec_rid}]({ml.cite(exec_rid)}), "
        f"asset [{asset_path.asset_rid}]({ml.cite(asset_path.asset_rid)}))"
    )

# Sanity check: each experiment must have produced its own bytes. If
# two experiments end up byte-identical, downstream metrics will be
# silently wrong, so fail loudly here.
if len(experiments) > 1:
    import hashlib

    fingerprints = {
        exp['asset_rid']: hashlib.md5(Path(exp['csv_path']).read_bytes()).hexdigest()
        for exp in experiments
    }
    distinct = len(set(fingerprints.values()))
    assert distinct == len(experiments), (
        f"Multiple experiments returned identical prediction CSVs: "
        f"{fingerprints}. Each experiment should have a distinct "
        f"prediction CSV; identical bytes mean two experiments are "
        f"reading the same file."
    )

display(Markdown("**Loaded experiments:**\n\n" + "\n".join(loaded_info)))
display(Markdown(f"\n*Successfully loaded {len(experiments)} experiments*"))
```

## Experiment Summary

Display detailed configuration information for each experiment using the `Experiment` class. This shows:
- **Configuration Choices**: The Hydra config names used (e.g., `model_config=cifar10_quick`)
- **Model Configuration**: Hyperparameters like epochs, learning rate, batch size
- **Input Datasets**: Training and test datasets used
- **Input Assets**: Any pre-trained weights or other assets used as inputs

### cifar10_quick ([XDP](https://localhost/chaise/record/#96/deriva-ml:Execution/RID=XDP))
**Description:** Quick CIFAR-10 training: 3 epochs, 32->64 channels, batch size 128 for fast validation
**Configuration Choices:** `assets=default_asset`, `datasets=cifar10_small_labeled_split`, `deriva_ml=default_deriva`, `experiment=cifar10_quick`, `model_config=cifar10_quick`, `script_config=none`, `workflow=default_workflow`
**Model Configuration:**
- **batch_size**: 128
- **conv1_channels**: 32
- **conv2_channels**: 64
- **description**: Quick training config: 3 epochs, batch 128. Use for rapid iteration, debugging, and verifying the training pipeline works correctly.
- **dropout_rate**: 0.0
- **epochs**: 3
- **execution**: None
- **hidden_size**: 128
- **learning_rate**: 0.001
- **ml_instance**: None
- **seed**: 42
- **test_only**: False
- **weight_decay**: 0.0
- **weights_filename**: cifar10_cnn_weights.pt
**Input Datasets:**
- [WD2](https://localhost/chaise/record/#96/deriva-ml:Dataset/RID=WD2) v0.1.0.post1.dev1 [Split]: Small CIFAR-10 labeled split: stratified 400/100 from training (seed=42).
  - [WDA](https://localhost/chaise/record/#96/deriva-ml:Dataset/RID=WDA) v0.1.0.post1.dev1 [Training, Labeled]: Training subset (400 samples) of JZT (stratified by Execution_Image_Image_Classification.Image_Class, seed=42)
  - [WDM](https://localhost/chaise/record/#96/deriva-ml:Dataset/RID=WDM) v0.1.0.post1.dev1 [Testing, Labeled]: Testing subset (100 samples) of JZT (stratified by Execution_Image_Image_Classification.Image_Class, seed=42)

### default_model ([XPR](https://localhost/chaise/record/#96/deriva-ml:Execution/RID=XPR))
**Description:** Simple model run
**Configuration Choices:** `assets=default_asset`, `datasets=cifar10_small_labeled_split`, `deriva_ml=default_deriva`, `model_config=default_model`, `script_config=none`, `workflow=default_workflow`
**Model Configuration:**
- **batch_size**: 64
- **conv1_channels**: 32
- **conv2_channels**: 64
- **description**: Default CIFAR-10 CNN: 32→64 channels, 128 hidden units, 10 epochs, batch size 64, lr=1e-3. Balanced config for standard training runs.
- **dropout_rate**: 0.0
- **epochs**: 10
- **execution**: None
- **hidden_size**: 128
- **learning_rate**: 0.001
- **ml_instance**: None
- **seed**: 42
- **test_only**: False
- **weight_decay**: 0.0
- **weights_filename**: cifar10_cnn_weights.pt
**Input Datasets:**

## Get Ground Truth Labels

Retrieve ground truth labels from the `Image_Classification` feature table. This feature stores classification labels for images, potentially from multiple sources (executions).

**Identifying ground truth:**
- Ground truth labels are manually assigned and have **no confidence score** (NULL)
- Model predictions have confidence scores from softmax probabilities
- We identify the ground truth execution by finding labels with zero confidence values


```python
# Get ground truth labels from the feature table
all_feature_values = [r.model_dump() for r in ml.feature_values("Image", "Image_Classification")]
feature_df = pd.DataFrame(all_feature_values)

# Ground truth labels have no confidence score (manually labeled)
# Group by execution to identify which has ground truth
exec_summary = feature_df.groupby('Execution').agg({
    'Image': 'count',
    'Confidence': lambda x: x.notna().sum()
}).rename(columns={'Image': 'num_images', 'Confidence': 'with_confidence'})

# Find execution with no confidence scores (ground truth)
gt_mask = exec_summary['with_confidence'] == 0
if gt_mask.any():
    gt_execution = exec_summary[gt_mask].index[0]
else:
    gt_execution = exec_summary['num_images'].idxmax()

# Build the ground-truth lookup from rows that match BOTH the
# ground-truth execution AND a null Confidence. The combined filter
# is the contract downstream code assumes; the assertion guards
# against the case where the same Image RID appears more than once in
# the GT execution (later rows would silently overwrite earlier ones
# through dict(zip(...))).
gt_rows = feature_df[
    (feature_df['Execution'] == gt_execution) & feature_df['Confidence'].isna()
][['Image', 'Image_Class']]

# Extract ground truth as lookup dictionary
gt_lookup = dict(zip(gt_rows['Image'], gt_rows['Image_Class']))

assert len(gt_lookup) == len(gt_rows), (
    f"gt_lookup has {len(gt_lookup)} entries but the ground-truth "
    f"feature subset has {len(gt_rows)} rows; check the filter "
    f"(Execution=={gt_execution} AND Confidence IS NULL) and look "
    f"for duplicate Image RIDs in the GT execution."
)

# Get class names
class_names = sorted(gt_rows['Image_Class'].unique())
n_classes = len(class_names)

display(Markdown(f"""**Ground Truth:**
- Execution: [{gt_execution}]({ml.cite(gt_execution)})
- Total labels: {len(gt_rows)}
- Classes ({n_classes}): {', '.join(f'`{c}`' for c in class_names)}
"""))
```

## Merge Predictions with Ground Truth

Join prediction data with ground truth labels using `Image_RID` as the key. Only images that have both predictions and ground truth labels will be included in the ROC analysis.


```python
# Add ground truth to each experiment's predictions
merge_results = []

for exp in experiments:
    df = exp['data'].copy()
    
    # Map Image_RID to ground truth class
    df['True_Class'] = df['Image_RID'].map(gt_lookup)
    
    # Keep only images with ground truth
    matched = df['True_Class'].notna().sum()
    total = len(df)
    
    df = df.dropna(subset=['True_Class'])
    exp['data'] = df
    exp['n_samples'] = len(df)
    
    if len(df) > 0:
        exp['accuracy'] = (df['Predicted_Class'] == df['True_Class']).mean() * 100
        merge_results.append(f"- **{exp['name']}**: {matched}/{total} matched, accuracy {exp['accuracy']:.1f}%")
    else:
        exp['accuracy'] = float('nan')
        merge_results.append(f"- **{exp['name']}**: ⚠️ No matching samples found!")

display(Markdown("**Prediction/Ground Truth Merge:**\n\n" + "\n".join(merge_results)))
```

- **cifar10_quick**: 100/100 matched, accuracy 24.0%
- **default_model**: 100/100 matched, accuracy 38.0%
- **cifar10_extended**: 100/100 matched, accuracy 41.0%


## Compute ROC Curves

For multi-class classification, we use the **one-vs-rest (OvR)** approach:
- Each class gets its own ROC curve treating it as positive vs. all others
- **Micro-average**: Aggregate all classes, treating each prediction as independent
- **Macro-average**: Simple mean of per-class AUC scores (equal weight to each class)

AUC (Area Under ROC Curve) ranges from 0.5 (random) to 1.0 (perfect discrimination).


```python
def compute_roc_metrics(df: pd.DataFrame, class_names: list[str]) -> dict:
    """Compute ROC curves and AUC scores for multi-class predictions.
    
    Args:
        df: DataFrame with True_Class and prob_* columns
        class_names: Ordered list of class names
        
    Returns:
        Dict with fpr, tpr, roc_auc for each class and micro/macro averages
    """
    n_classes = len(class_names)
    class_to_idx = {name: i for i, name in enumerate(class_names)}
    
    # Convert labels to indices
    y_true_idx = df['True_Class'].map(class_to_idx).values
    y_true_bin = label_binarize(y_true_idx, classes=range(n_classes))
    
    # Get probability matrix
    prob_cols = [f"prob_{c}" for c in class_names]
    y_score = df[prob_cols].values
    
    # Compute per-class ROC
    fpr, tpr, roc_auc = {}, {}, {}
    for i, name in enumerate(class_names):
        fpr[name], tpr[name], _ = roc_curve(y_true_bin[:, i], y_score[:, i])
        roc_auc[name] = auc(fpr[name], tpr[name])
    
    # Micro-average
    fpr['micro'], tpr['micro'], _ = roc_curve(y_true_bin.ravel(), y_score.ravel())
    roc_auc['micro'] = auc(fpr['micro'], tpr['micro'])
    
    # Macro-average
    roc_auc['macro'] = np.mean([roc_auc[c] for c in class_names])
    
    return {'fpr': fpr, 'tpr': tpr, 'roc_auc': roc_auc}
```


```python
# Compute ROC metrics for each experiment
roc_results = []

for exp in experiments:
    metrics = compute_roc_metrics(exp['data'], class_names)
    exp.update(metrics)
    
    roc_results.append(
        f"- **{exp['name']}** (asset [{exp['asset_rid']}]({ml.cite(exp['asset_rid'])})): "
        f"Accuracy {exp['accuracy']:.2f}%, Micro-AUC {exp['roc_auc']['micro']:.4f}, "
        f"Macro-AUC {exp['roc_auc']['macro']:.4f}"
    )

display(Markdown("**ROC Metrics:**\n\n" + "\n".join(roc_results)))
```

## Plot ROC Curves


```python
def plot_roc_curves(exp: dict, class_names: list[str], show_per_class: bool = True):
    """Plot ROC curves for an experiment.
    
    Args:
        exp: Experiment dict with fpr, tpr, roc_auc data
        class_names: List of class names
        show_per_class: If True, plot individual class curves. If False, only micro-average.
    """
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(10, 8))
    
    fpr, tpr, roc_auc = exp['fpr'], exp['tpr'], exp['roc_auc']
    
    # Micro-average (always shown)
    ax.plot(fpr['micro'], tpr['micro'], 
            label=f"Micro-avg (AUC={roc_auc['micro']:.3f})",
            color='deeppink', linestyle=':', linewidth=3)
    
    # Per-class curves (optional based on config)
    if show_per_class:
        colors = plt.cm.tab10(np.linspace(0, 1, len(class_names)))
        for i, name in enumerate(class_names):
            ax.plot(fpr[name], tpr[name], color=colors[i],
                    label=f"{name} (AUC={roc_auc[name]:.3f})")
    
    ax.plot([0, 1], [0, 1], 'k--', alpha=0.5)
    ax.set_xlim([0, 1])
    ax.set_ylim([0, 1.05])
    ax.set_xlabel('False Positive Rate')
    ax.set_ylabel('True Positive Rate')
    
    # Use experiment name in title
    exp_name = exp.get('name', exp['asset_rid'])
    ax.set_title(f"ROC Curves: {exp_name} (Acc: {exp['accuracy']:.1f}%)")
    
    ax.legend(loc='lower right', fontsize=9)
    ax.grid(True, alpha=0.3)
    
    return fig
```


```python
# Plot ROC curves for each experiment and save as JPEG assets.
#
# The output filename includes the prediction CSV's asset RID, not just
# ``exp['name']``. ``exp['name']`` resolves to the Hydra
# ``model_config`` name (deriva-ml ``Experiment.name``), so a sweep
# that varies only learning rate or batch size leaves every cell with
# the same name; without the asset_rid suffix, sweep cells silently
# overwrite each other's ROC JPEGs.
for exp in experiments:
    fig = plot_roc_curves(exp, class_names, show_per_class=config.show_per_class)
    plt.tight_layout()

    # Save as execution asset, disambiguated by the prediction CSV's RID.
    exp_name = exp.get('name', exp['asset_rid']).replace(' ', '_')
    roc_path = execution.asset_file_path(
        MLAsset.execution_asset,
        f"roc_curves_{exp_name}_{exp['asset_rid']}.jpg",
        ExecAssetType.output_file,
    )
    fig.savefig(roc_path, format='jpeg', dpi=150, bbox_inches='tight')
    display(Markdown(f"*Saved: `{roc_path.name}`*"))

    plt.show()
```

   


## Experiment Comparison

Compare micro-averaged ROC curves across all experiments. This visualization shows how different model configurations perform relative to each other:
- Curves closer to the top-left corner indicate better performance
- The diagonal dashed line represents random classification (AUC = 0.5)


```python
# Compare micro-average ROC curves across experiments and save the
# overlay as an asset of the analysis execution. There's only one
# comparison plot per analysis run, so the filename is keyed on the
# analysis execution RID (not the loaded-experiment RIDs).
if len(experiments) > 1:
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(10, 8))
    colors = plt.cm.Set1(np.linspace(0, 1, len(experiments)))

    for i, exp in enumerate(experiments):
        exp_name = exp.get('name', exp['asset_rid'])
        # Include the prediction CSV's RID in the legend so sweep cells
        # that share a Hydra model_config name are still
        # distinguishable on the comparison plot.
        label = (
            f"{exp_name} [{exp['asset_rid']}] "
            f"(AUC={exp['roc_auc']['micro']:.3f}, Acc={exp['accuracy']:.1f}%)"
        )
        ax.plot(exp['fpr']['micro'], exp['tpr']['micro'],
                color=colors[i], linewidth=2, label=label)

    ax.plot([0, 1], [0, 1], 'k--', alpha=0.5, label='Random')
    ax.set_xlim([0, 1])
    ax.set_ylim([0, 1.05])
    ax.set_xlabel('False Positive Rate')
    ax.set_ylabel('True Positive Rate')
    ax.set_title('ROC Curve Comparison (Micro-Average)')
    ax.legend(loc='lower right', fontsize=8)
    ax.grid(True, alpha=0.3)
    plt.tight_layout()

    comparison_path = execution.asset_file_path(
        MLAsset.execution_asset,
        f"roc_comparison_{execution.execution_rid}.jpg",
        ExecAssetType.output_file,
    )
    fig.savefig(comparison_path, format='jpeg', dpi=150, bbox_inches='tight')
    display(Markdown(f"*Saved: `{comparison_path.name}`*"))

    plt.show()
else:
    display(Markdown("*Single experiment - no comparison plot*"))
```

   


## Confusion Matrices

Confusion matrices show the distribution of predicted vs. actual classes. Each row represents the true class, and each column represents the predicted class. Values along the diagonal are correct predictions. Normalized by row to show per-class recall rates.


```python
from sklearn.metrics import confusion_matrix

def plot_confusion_matrix(exp: dict, class_names: list[str]):
    """Plot normalized confusion matrix for an experiment.

    Args:
        exp: Experiment dict with 'data' DataFrame containing True_Class and Predicted_Class
        class_names: Ordered list of class names
    """
    df = exp['data']
    cm = confusion_matrix(df['True_Class'], df['Predicted_Class'], labels=class_names)

    # Normalize by row (true class) to get recall per class
    cm_norm = cm.astype(float) / cm.sum(axis=1, keepdims=True)

    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(10, 8))
    im = ax.imshow(cm_norm, interpolation='nearest', cmap='Blues', vmin=0, vmax=1)
    fig.colorbar(im, ax=ax, label='Recall')

    # Label ticks
    ax.set_xticks(range(len(class_names)))
    ax.set_yticks(range(len(class_names)))
    ax.set_xticklabels(class_names, rotation=45, ha='right', fontsize=9)
    ax.set_yticklabels(class_names, fontsize=9)

    # Annotate cells with count and percentage
    thresh = cm_norm.max() / 2.0
    for i in range(len(class_names)):
        for j in range(len(class_names)):
            color = 'white' if cm_norm[i, j] > thresh else 'black'
            ax.text(j, i, f"{cm[i, j]}\n({cm_norm[i, j]:.1%})",
                    ha='center', va='center', color=color, fontsize=8)

    ax.set_xlabel('Predicted Class')
    ax.set_ylabel('True Class')

    exp_name = exp.get('name', exp['asset_rid'])
    ax.set_title(f"Confusion Matrix: {exp_name} (Acc: {exp['accuracy']:.1f}%)")

    return fig

# Plot confusion matrix for each experiment and save as JPEG assets.
#
# Suffix with the prediction CSV's asset RID (same reason as the ROC
# plot cell above): sweep cells share a Hydra model_config name and
# would otherwise overwrite each other.
for exp in experiments:
    fig = plot_confusion_matrix(exp, class_names)
    plt.tight_layout()

    exp_name = exp.get('name', exp['asset_rid']).replace(' ', '_')
    cm_path = execution.asset_file_path(
        MLAsset.execution_asset,
        f"confusion_matrix_{exp_name}_{exp['asset_rid']}.jpg",
        ExecAssetType.output_file,
    )
    fig.savefig(cm_path, format='jpeg', dpi=150, bbox_inches='tight')
    display(Markdown(f"*Saved: `{cm_path.name}`*"))

    plt.show()

```

   


## Export ROC Metrics

Save per-class and aggregate AUC scores as a CSV for downstream analysis or comparison across runs.


```python
# Build ROC metrics DataFrame and save as CSV asset.
#
# The DataFrame includes both Execution_RID (the producing run) and
# Asset_RID (the specific prediction CSV that was loaded). When two
# sweep cells share the same Hydra ``model_config`` name, Asset_RID
# is the only field that distinguishes their rows.
rows = []
for exp in experiments:
    exp_name = exp.get('name', exp['asset_rid'])
    exp_obj = exp.get('experiment')
    row = {
        'Experiment': exp_name,
        'Asset_RID': exp['asset_rid'],
        'Execution_RID': exp_obj.execution_rid if exp_obj else '',
        'Samples': exp['n_samples'],
        'Accuracy': round(exp['accuracy'], 4),
    }
    for c in class_names:
        row[f'AUC_{c}'] = round(exp['roc_auc'][c], 6)
    row['AUC_Micro'] = round(exp['roc_auc']['micro'], 6)
    row['AUC_Macro'] = round(exp['roc_auc']['macro'], 6)
    rows.append(row)

metrics_df = pd.DataFrame(rows)

csv_path = execution.asset_file_path(
    MLAsset.execution_asset, "roc_metrics.csv", ExecAssetType.output_file
)
metrics_df.to_csv(csv_path, index=False)

display(Markdown(f"*Saved: `{csv_path.name}`*"))
display(metrics_df)

```


*Saved: `roc_metrics.csv`*

## Summary


```python
# Final summary
summary_lines = [
    "# CIFAR-10 ROC Analysis Summary",
    "",
    f"**Catalog:** `{ml.host_name}:{ml.catalog_id}`",
    f"**Execution:** [{execution.execution_rid}]({ml.cite(execution.execution_rid)})",
    f"**Ground truth:** Execution [{gt_execution}]({ml.cite(gt_execution)}) ({len(gt_lookup)} labels)",
    f"**Classes:** {n_classes}",
    f"**Experiments analyzed:** {len(experiments)}",
]

display(Markdown("\n".join(summary_lines)))

# Display asset configuration description if available
if hasattr(config.assets, 'description') and config.assets.description:
    display(Markdown(f"\n**Analysis Context:**\n\n{config.assets.description}"))

# Display per-experiment summary
results_lines = ["## Experiment Results", ""]
for exp in experiments:
    exp_name = exp.get('name', exp['asset_rid'])
    exp_obj = exp.get('experiment')
    
    # Get experiment description if available
    exp_desc = ""
    if exp_obj and exp_obj.description:
        exp_desc = f" - {exp_obj.description}"
    
    # Link to source execution
    exec_link = f"[{exp_obj.execution_rid}]({ml.cite(exp_obj.execution_rid)})" if exp_obj else exp['asset_rid']
    
    results_lines.append(f"### {exp_name}{exp_desc}")
    results_lines.append(f"- **Execution:** {exec_link}")
    results_lines.append(f"- **Samples:** {exp['n_samples']}")
    results_lines.append(f"- **Accuracy:** {exp['accuracy']:.2f}%")
    results_lines.append(f"- **Micro-AUC:** {exp['roc_auc']['micro']:.4f}")
    results_lines.append(f"- **Macro-AUC:** {exp['roc_auc']['macro']:.4f}")
    results_lines.append("")

display(Markdown("\n".join(results_lines)))
```

## Experiment Results

### cifar10_quick - Quick CIFAR-10 training: 3 epochs, 32->64 channels, batch size 128 for fast validation
- **Execution:** [XDP](https://localhost/id/96/XDP@355-HSE6-J0ZR)
- **Samples:** 100
- **Accuracy:** 24.00%
- **Micro-AUC:** 0.6773
- **Macro-AUC:** 0.7124

### default_model - Simple model run
- **Execution:** [XPR](https://localhost/id/96/XPR@355-HSE6-J0ZR)
- **Samples:** 100
- **Accuracy:** 38.00%
- **Micro-AUC:** 0.7874
- **Macro-AUC:** 0.7959

### cifar10_extended - Extended CIFAR-10 training: 50 epochs, 64->128 channels, dropout 0.25, weight decay 1e-4
- **Execution:** [XZT](https://localhost/id/96/XZT@355-HSE6-J0ZR)
- **Samples:** 100
- **Accuracy:** 41.00%
- **Micro-AUC:** 0.7979
- **Macro-AUC:** 0.8046
